# **ChainTorrent MVP**

## Problem Statement

JavaScript dependency distribution runs through a single centralized registry that is an availability chokepoint, a mutable source of truth, and an intermediary between the people who publish packages and the people who consume them. ChainTorrent's MVP replaces that path with a content-addressed swarm, a canonical on-chain identity registry, and transferable access entitlements, so that the distribution and identity model is proven with monetization out of bounds.

**Adoption runs individual developer first, and everything else follows from that.** A developer at a terminal gains cross-project reuse of whatever their machine has already fetched, swarm retrieval on popular packages, and installs that survive a registry outage. Build platforms adopt next and for their own reasons: a service running the same few thousand popular installs continuously, on always-on machines with real bandwidth, holds the ideal cache as a by-product of its own economics, and seeding that cache costs almost nothing once it exists. That makes them natural superseeders rather than participants anyone has to recruit, and it is why no privileged bootstrap host is part of this plan. CI and enterprise adoption arrive as a consequence of that sequence rather than as targets to be won early.

The sequence matters to this workplan because it determines what the MVP measures. Latency is judged against interactive local installs rather than pipeline tolerance, since a developer notices a doubled install where a pipeline does not. Cache reuse is the headline adoption metric, because it is the benefit the adopting population actually experiences. Registry-outage survival remains true throughout and is simply not the lead, since it matters most to the population that adopts last.

The design is specified in `docs/research/cryptography.md` and bounded in `docs/research/MVP Scope.md`. Undetermined decisions are held in the To-Do list below.

The cryptographic construction is specified; its research record is `docs/research/cryptography-research-notebook.md`, and its statement is `docs/research/cryptography-critical-path.md`. A validation harness on both pairing curves produces the sizes, timings, and gas against the configured piece-group size and curve defaults, and nothing waits on it: every node reads the piece-group size from the deployment record and depends only on the code it calls.

## Objectives

Deliver an install path for JavaScript dependencies that survives registry outages, serves popular packages from a peer swarm, and exercises the identity and entitlement pipeline end to end.

Prove distribution and identity. Willingness to pay and seeder compensation are out of bounds and are held in the To-Do list; the transaction flow is proven at a nominal price on assets the project publishes, as a test instrument rather than a monetization step.

Every undetermined decision is carried in the To-Do list, which every workplan that stems from this one inherits.

## Expected Outcome

A developer installs dependencies and receives a working `node_modules` assembled from a local content-addressed store, a peer swarm, or the ingest source as fallback, with canonical identity resolved on-chain and an access entitlement minted to their wallet.

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/vectors/replay` **The replay vector set, generic over the credential KEM, the key agreement, and the delivery proof with its chain forms: per record a mint delivery from `harness-crypto/delivery/mint` over the typed mint context and the admitted identity, a transfer delivery from `harness-crypto/delivery/transfer` from the mint delivery's recipient to a buyer under the typed transfer context, and a transfer delivery from that buyer's holder to a next buyer under the typed next context, the next settlement of the same entitlement, each settlement's reference verdict, the reference verdict of the first transfer's proof against the next statement, and the deliveries' uniforms; admits the next context as the next settlement before any record; composes no delivery step, draws nothing itself, and admits no identity; owns the record, the settlement, verdict, and next-settlement field types, and the function's types**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: a delivery proof is valid for exactly one settlement, so a transfer proof replayed against the next settlement of the same entitlement must be refused by the reference verifier in both verifier forms; the harness holds, for each record, two consecutive transfer settlements of one entitlement with their statements and proofs, each settlement's verdict, the verdict of the first settlement's proof against the next settlement's statement, and the uniforms the record drew, as the replay vectors (CR-09 replay across settlements; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/vectors/replay` row and its conflict flag on the vectors as a composing function over functions it owns; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope, `harness-crypto/vectors` composed over `harness-crypto/vectors/replay`, and its exit, CR-09 in both verifier forms with replay across settlements rejected; `docs/research/cryptography.md`'s Replay Attacks, a captured envelope and proof verifying for exactly one settlement, and Phase 3's Delivery, the seller supplying the previous envelope as the proof's statement; `docs/research/cryptography-research-notebook.md`'s claim (v), non-malleability and replay)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the mint, transfer, and next contexts arrive typed, in the delivery proof's forms, each carrying its purpose, and the identity arrives admitted, paired with its setup scope; the function rebuilds no form and admits no identity
    * `[✅]`   Functional: before any record, the next context is admitted as the next settlement of the same entitlement only when, compared by the `to_field` values of their forms and by the bytes of their domain values, its chain identifier, entitlement contract, asset root, parameter-set identifier, source entitlement, and target entitlement equal the transfer context's, its old interval equals the transfer context's new interval, and its seller equals the transfer context's buyer; the first field that differs is refused with its `NextSettlementField`
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each record is a mint delivery over the mint context and the admitted identity; a transfer delivery over the transfer context, the mint delivery's parameter set and identity element, and its recipient as the holder; and a transfer delivery over the next context, the same parameter set and identity element, and the first transfer delivery's buyer as the holder; the record's transfer statement and proof are the first transfer delivery's own and its next statement and proof the second's
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each record verifies the transfer statement against its proof, the next statement against its proof, and the next statement against the transfer statement's proof, the replay
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every uniform has the length the pairing's scalar sampling bound declares; a record's draws are the mint delivery's six uniforms, the first transfer delivery's three, and the second's three, concatenated in that order; the function draws nothing itself; the envelope coins and the proof nonces are drawn inside their adapters and are not recorded
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the function returns the configured count of records in index order
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a refused next context is returned in the error arm with its field; a failed mint delivery and a failed transfer delivery, the latter with its settlement, are each returned unchanged in the error arm, the delivery's own error whole; a failed verification is returned unchanged with its verdict
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function reads no clock and touches no filesystem; it names no curve, concrete, form, scope, count, identity, purpose, or context value; the count arrives in its params and the typed contexts and the admitted identity in its payload

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/vectors` **The composing function over the vector sets the harness's vectors own: calls `cross_holder`, `rerandomization`, `non_convertibility`, `malformed_capsules`, `cross_set`, `statement_mutations`, and `replay`, in that order, each with its configured count, over the borrowed pairing, credential KEM, key agreement, delivery proof with its chain forms, randomness source, key-derivation adapter, and encoder its caller hands it, and over what each set takes of the admitted identity and other identity, the configured derivation context, and the typed mint, transfer, and next contexts, and returns every set's records; owns the sets' success and error types**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the harness holds the algebraic and mutation vector sets, each produced by a function the vectors own over adapters its caller obtained through their factories, generic over the form interface and apart from the generate concrete's parity inputs; one call composes them, handing each set its configured count and what that set takes of the admitted identities and the configured and typed contexts, and returns every set together or the first set's refusal (CR-08, CR-09, CR-11 vectors; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/vectors` row, the composing function calling each set with its configured count over the adapters and declarations its caller hands it and owning the sets' success and error types, and its conflict flag on the vectors as a composing function over functions it owns, each function owning its record type and the composing function the sets' return; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope, `harness-crypto/vectors`, the algebraic and mutation vector sets generic over the form interface, apart from the generate concrete's parity inputs, composed over `cross_holder`, `rerandomization`, `non_convertibility`, `malformed_capsules`, `cross_set`, `statement_mutations`, and `replay`, and its exit, CR-09 in both verifier forms with every statement field and response mutated and replay across settlements rejected; the technical requirements' file tree, `vectors` a composing function over the functions it owns beneath it, each a module directory holding one vector set's function and record type)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the function calls each set's function once, in the order `cross_holder`, `rerandomization`, `non_convertibility`, `malformed_capsules`, `cross_set`, `statement_mutations`, `replay`, the order the dependency map's row and the milestone's scope list them
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the identities arrive admitted, each an `AdmittedKemIdentity` paired with its setup scope, and the delivery contexts typed, in the delivery proof's forms, each carrying its purpose; the function admits nothing and rebuilds no form
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each set receives its own configured count; the admitted identity reaches every set; the admitted other identity reaches `non_convertibility` alone; the configured derivation context reaches `cross_set` alone; the typed mint and transfer contexts reach `statement_mutations` and `replay`; the typed next settlement's context reaches `replay` alone
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every set draws from the one randomness source the caller hands, in call order
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each set's success return is held unchanged in its own field of the success; a set's refusal is returned unchanged inside that set's variant, and no later set is called
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function reads no clock and touches no filesystem; it names no curve, concrete, form, scope, count, identity, purpose, or context value; the counts arrive in its params and the admitted identities, the derivation context, and the typed delivery contexts in its payload

  * `[✅]`   `role`
    * `[✅]`   App module in the adapter role: the composing function of the `vectors` module of `apps/harness-crypto`, beneath which the set functions it owns live
    * `[✅]`   Owns `VectorsDeps`, `VectorsParams`, `VectorsPayload`, `VectorsSuccessReturn`, `VectorsErrorReturn`, `VectorsReturn`, and `VectorsFn`; consumes each set's function with its deps, params, payload, success, and error types from that set's node, `VectorCount`, which `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/group_vectors` owns, and `AdmittedKemIdentity`, which `harness-crypto/delivery/mint` owns
    * `[✅]`   Writes `apps/harness-crypto/src/vectors/mod.rs` and `apps/harness-crypto/src/vectors/provides.rs`, which `harness-crypto/vectors/cross_holder` creates and each set extends, adding the function's module declarations, the function, and its re-export lines, so the function is a public entry of the crate through the crate barrel
    * `[✅]`   Composes; does not set up, issue, rerandomize, encapsulate, decapsulate, wrap, unwrap, prove, or verify, which each set's function does through the adapters it is handed
    * `[✅]`   Does not choose a count, a scope, an identity, a derivation context, a delivery context, or a purpose, and admits no identity or context; the configuration carries them, the KEM factory admits the scope, `harness-crypto/config/admit_statements` admits each identity, `harness-crypto/run/kem_consumer` pairs each with its setup scope as an `AdmittedKemIdentity`, `harness-crypto/run/admit_contexts` admits each delivery context into the forms, and the caller passes them here
    * `[✅]`   Does not construct a pairing, KEM, key agreement, delivery proof, randomness source, key-derivation adapter, encoder, or forms; the caller obtains each through its factory or `chain/create_chain_forms` and hands this function the borrowed adapters
    * `[✅]`   Does not encode, report, write, submit, or measure a record
    * `[✅]`   Does not carry a commit

    * `[✅]`   Problem: the piece-group size and curve defaults are adjusted from measurement, so the harness holds, for each curve it runs, the cost of every cryptographic operation a client performs and the bytes and gas of every object a settlement carries: the time of proving and verifying each relation and of decapsulating a capsule, the bytes of a capsule, an envelope, and each relation's proof, and the L2 execution gas and L1 data fee of a mint and a transfer the deployed verifier accepts (CD-07 and AS-21's capsule, envelope, and proof sizes, decapsulation time per piece group, proof generation and verification time, and delivery cost as L2 execution gas and L1 data fee; RO-03's decapsulation time per group and proof generation and verification time and gas; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/measure` row, its Dependencies edges, and its conflict flags on the harness's mint and transfer each having one owner, on a measure taking its timing inputs from the delivery functions and not from the vector sets, and on a verifier client belonging to the harness's own family; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope for `harness-crypto/measure`, size, timing, and gas capture per curve, and its exit, CD-07 and AS-21; `docs/research/cryptography-critical-path.md`'s costs by formula, the quantities the measurement replaces)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the adapters, the typed mint and transfer contexts, and the admitted identity arrive handed; the function rebuilds no form of a context, admits no identity, constructs no adapter, and draws only the uniform each capsule is encapsulated from
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each iteration holds a mint delivery over the mint context and the admitted identity, a transfer delivery over the transfer context, the mint delivery's parameter set and identity element, and its recipient as the holder, and a capsule encapsulated under the mint delivery's parameter set from a uniform of the length the pairing's scalar sampling bound declares; none of the three is timed
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each of `prove_mint`, `prove_transfer`, `verify` over the mint statement and proof, `verify` over the transfer statement and proof, and `decapsulate` is timed alone, the clock read immediately before the call and immediately after it, over inputs built before the clock is read; a prover is handed copies of the witnesses its delivery returned, each secret copied through `Secret::try_new` over a clone of its exposed value, since a prover consumes what it is given
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the timing of each operation is the sum of its calls' elapsed times divided by the iteration count, so it is the mean time of one call, returned with the iteration count it was computed from
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the byte sizes are those of one mint delivery, the transfer delivery that follows it, and a capsule encapsulated under the mint delivery's parameter set, each object's group elements and scalars encoded through the pairing's `encode_g1`, `encode_g2`, and `encode_scalar` and their byte lengths summed: the capsule through `capsule_components`, the envelope the mint delivery's recipient holds through `envelope_components`, and each relation's proof through `proof_components`, its challenge, its responses, and, where the proof carries them, its second-group first messages
    * `[✅]`   Functional: that mint delivery and transfer delivery are each submitted once through the verifier concrete as `verify_on_chain` over the delivery's own statement and proof, the mint first; an accepted delivery's cost is its L2 execution gas and L1 data fee; a rejected or a reverted delivery is returned with its relation; the verifier's own refusal is returned unchanged with its relation
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a failed delivery, draw, encapsulation, prove, or verification is returned unchanged in the error arm, the callee's error whole, before any later call, and a verification or submission failure is returned with the relation it concerned
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function names no curve, concrete, form, scope, count, identity, endpoint, address, or context value; the iteration count arrives in its params, the adapters in its deps, and the typed contexts and the admitted identity in its payload; it reads the monotonic clock and touches no filesystem and no chain but through the verifier concrete it is handed

* `[ ]`   `harness-crypto/report` **The release-evidence report of one curve's delivery measurement: a JSON document recording the capsule, envelope, and proof sizes in bytes, the mean time of proving, verifying, and decapsulating, the L2 execution gas and L1 data fee of a mint and a transfer, the piece-group size and primary curve defaults in force, any adjustment the data supports, and the conditions the measurement was taken under, selecting nothing against a threshold, returned and written to each destination its params carry; composes no measurement and decides no default; owns the report's text, destination, defaults, adjustment, and function types**

  * `[ ]`   `objective`
    * `[ ]`   Problem: the piece-group size and curve defaults are adjusted from measurement, and the release evidence records what was measured, the defaults in force, and any adjustment the data supports, so the harness holds, for each curve it runs, one document that states the measurement `harness-crypto/measure` returned together with the defaults and the conditions it was taken under (CD-07 and AS-21's capsule, envelope, and proof sizes, decapsulation time per piece group, proof generation and verification time, and delivery cost as L2 execution gas and L1 data fee, with the piece-group size default in force and any adjustment the measurements support recorded in release evidence; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/report` row, its Dependencies edge on `harness-crypto/measure`, and its statement that the report selects nothing against a threshold; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope for `harness-crypto/report`, the release-evidence report written under the destinations its params carry, and its exit, the measurements, the piece-group size and curve defaults in force, and any adjustment the data supports recorded in release evidence; the technical requirements' Harness report, a release-evidence JSON document per curve; `docs/project-planning/success-metrics.md`'s Measurement Plan, the conditions declaring that the L1 fee is the test network's and is re-taken on the mainnet pilot; `docs/research/MVP Scope.md`'s Cost Instrumentation, the defaults adjusted when the data shows a better value and no threshold failing a run)
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the measurement, the curve's label, the conditions, the defaults in force, and the adjustment arrive handed and admitted; the function computes, compares, and selects nothing, and records each value as it was handed
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the document is a JSON object holding the curve's label, the conditions text, the iteration count the means were computed from, the five timings, the four sizes in bytes, the two delivery costs, the defaults in force, and the adjustment or its absence
    * `[ ]`   Functional: each timing is recorded as the whole seconds and the subsecond nanoseconds of the `Duration` the measurement holds, so no precision is lost and no conversion can fail
    * `[ ]`   Functional: each delivery's cost is recorded as its L2 execution gas and its L1 data fee, the fee as the state the chain returned it in: a charged fee with its wei as decimal text, since the wei is a `u128`, and a fee the chain does not charge as its own state, never as a zero
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the document is returned and is written, as the same text, to each destination the params carry in the order the params carry them, a file at its path or standard output; a refused write is returned unchanged with the destination it concerned, before any later destination is written
    * `[ ]`   Non-functional: the function names no curve, concrete, form, count, network, path, file name, default, or threshold; the destinations arrive in its params and everything recorded arrives in its payload; it compares no recorded value with any bound, creates no directory, and touches the filesystem and standard output only to write the document to the destinations it was handed
